permit, when such Industrial User violates federal or state laws or regulations relating to wastewater 
discharge, any provision of this Chapter, or any permit or order issued hereunder. Suspensions or revocations 
of permits, and terminations, suspensions, or disconnections of water and/or sewer service, shall be made 
according to the following procedure:  
1.   The Industrial User, facility owner, or landowner shall be notified by the BOPU by certified mail at least 
seven days prior to suspending or revoking a permit, or terminating, suspending, or disconnecting water 
and/or sewer service. Such notice shall set forth with particularity the reasons such action is 
contemplated.  
2.   Upon receipt of notice, the Industrial User, facility owner, or landowner may request a hearing before the 
BOPU. Such request shall be in writing and received by the BOPU within seven days of receipt of the 
notice. Failure of the Industrial User, facility owner, or landowner to request a hearing within seven days 
shall result in a waiver of the right to a hearing.  
3.   If a hearing is requested, the BOPU shall set a hearing time, date, and location. The BOPU shall notify the 
requesting party in writing by certified mail of the time, date, and location of the hearing.  
4.    Hearings shall be conducted in accordance with the Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act.  
5.    If the BOPU has reason to believe that an Industrial User is threatening the health, safety and welfare of 
the public, environment, or the operation of the POTW, the BOPU may immediately and without notice 
enter an order suspending or revoking a permit, or terminating, suspending, or disconnecting water 
and/or sewer service. Notice of the order, either verbal or written, shall be served on the Industrial User, 
setting forth with particularity the grounds relied upon for the action taken. The Industrial User may, 
upon receipt of notice, request a hearing before the BOPU and the hearing shall be conducted within two 
city business days.
Article 7. - Enforcement—Judicial Remedies  
 
13.20.750 - Injunctive relief.  
 
When the control authority BOPU finds that an Industrial User has violated, or continues to violate, any 
provision of this Chapter, an Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or 
any other Pretreatment Standard or Requirement, the control authority BOPU may petition the appropriate court in 
the state of Wyoming for the issuance of a temporary or permanent injunction, as appropriate, which restrains or 
compels the specific performance of the Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, order, or other requirement 
imposed by this Chapter on activities of the Industrial User. The control authority BOPU may also seek such other 
legal or equitable relief, including a requirement for the user to conduct environmental remediation. A petition for 
injunctive relief shall not be a bar against, or a prerequisite for, taking any other action against an Industrial User.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
13.20.760 - Civil penalties.  
 

 
 
A.   An Industrial User who has violated, or continues to violate, any provision of this Chapter, an Individual 
Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or any other Pretreatment Standard 
or Requirement shall be liable to the BOPU for a maximum civil penalty of five ten thousand dollars 
($510,000.00) per violation, per day. In the case of a monthly or other long-term average discharge limit, 
penalties shall accrue for each day during the period of the violation.  
B.   The control authority BOPU may recover reasonable attorneys' fees, court costs, and other expenses 
associated with enforcement activities, including sampling and monitoring expenses, and the cost of any 
actual damages incurred by the BOPU.  
C.  
In determining the amount of civil liability, the court shall take into account all relevant circumstances, 
including, but not limited to, the extent of harm caused by the violation, the magnitude and duration of the 
violation, any economic benefit gained through the Industrial User's violation, corrective actions by the 
Industrial User, the compliance history of the Industrial User, and any other factor as justice requires.  
D.   Filing a suit for civil penalties shall not be a bar against, or a prerequisite for, taking any other action against 
an Industrial User.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
13.20.770 - Criminal prosecution.  
 
A.   An Industrial User who willfully or negligently violates any provision of this Chapter, an Individual 
Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or any other Pretreatment Standard 
or Requirement shall, upon conviction, be guilty of a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine of not more than 
seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00) per violation, per day, or imprisonment for not more than six months, or 
both.  
B.  
An Industrial User who knowingly makes any false statements, representations, or certifications in any 
application, record, report, plan, or other documentation filed, or required to be maintained, pursuant to this 
Chapter, Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or who falsifies, 
tampers with, or knowingly renders inaccurate any monitoring device or method required under this Chapter 
shall, upon conviction, be guilty of a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine of not more than seven hundred fifty 
dollars ($750.00) per violation, per day, or imprisonment for not more than six months, or both.  
C.   An Industrial User who willfully or negligently introduces any substance into the POTW which causes 
personal injury or property damage shall, upon conviction, be guilty of a misdemeanor, punishable by a fine 
of not more than seven hundred fifty dollars ($750.00) per violation, per day, or imprisonment for not more 
than six months, or both. This penalty shall be in addition to any other cause of action for personal injury or 
property damage available under other applicable law.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
13.20.780 - Remedies—Nonexclusive.  
 
The remedies provided for in this Chapter are not exclusive. The control authority BOPU may take any, all, 
or any combination of these actions against a noncompliant Industrial User. Enforcement of pretreatment violations 
will generally be in accordance with the BOPU Water Reclamation Division's Enforcement Response Plan. 
However, the control authority BOPU may take other action against any Industrial User when the circumstances 
warrant. Further, the control authority BOPU is empowered to take more than one multiple enforcement actions 
against any noncompliant Industrial User.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
Article 8. - Enforcement—Supplemental Actions  
 
13.20.790 - Penalties for late reports.  

 
 
A.  A penalty not to exceed five thousand dollars ($5,000.00) shall be assessed to any user for each day that a 
report required by this Chapter, or a permit or order issued hereunder is late, beginning five days after the date 
the report is due. Higher penalties may also be assessed where reports are more than thirty (30) days late. 
Actions taken by the control authority to collect late reporting penalties shall not limit the control authority's 
authority to initiate other enforcement actions that may include penalties for late reporting violations.  
B.  Users desiring to dispute such penalties must file a written request with the control authority to reconsider the 
penalty along with full payment of the penalty amount within ten days of being notified of the penalty. The 
control authority shall convene a hearing on the matter. In the event the user's appeal is successful, the payment 
together with any interest accrued at the prevailing rate shall be reimbursed to the user. In the event the appeal 
is unsuccessful, the control authority may add the costs of preparing administrative enforcement actions, such 
as notices and orders, to the penalty amount.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13)  
13.20.790 – RESERVED 
 
13.20.800 - Liability insurance.  
 
The control authority BOPU may decline to issue or reissue an Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit or 
general permit to any Industrial User who has failed to comply with any provision of this Chapter, a previous 
Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or any other Pretreatment 
Standard or Requirement, unless the Industrial User first submits proof that it has obtained financial assurances 
sufficient to restore or repair damage to the POTW caused by its discharge.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
13.20.810 - Payment of outstanding fees and penalties.  
 
The control authority BOPU may decline to issue or reissue an Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit or 
general permit to any Industrial User who has failed to pay any outstanding fees, fines or penalties incurred as a 
result of any provision of this Chapter, a previous Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order 
issued hereunder.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
13.20.820 - Water supply severance.  
 
Whenever an Industrial User has violated or continues to violate any provision of this Chapter, an 
Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit, general permit or order issued hereunder, or any other Pretreatment 
Standard or Requirement, water service to the Industrial User may be severed. Service will recommence, at the 
Industrial User's expense, only after the Industrial User has satisfactorily demonstrated its ability to comply and has 
paid all invoices, fines, and fees.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
Article 9. - Affirmative Defenses to Discharge Violations  
 
13.20.830 - Prohibited discharge standards.  
 
A.   A Industrial User shall have an affirmative defense to an enforcement action brought against it for 
noncompliance with the general prohibitions in Section 13.20.050 (A) of this Chapter or the specific 
prohibitions in Sections 13.20.050(B)(1)-(16) of this Chapter if it can prove that it did not know, or have 
reason to know, that its discharge, alone or in conjunction with discharges from other sources, would cause 
Pass Through or Interference and that either:  

 
 
1.  A local limit exists for each pollutant discharged and the Industrial User was in compliance with each 
limit directly prior to, and during, the Pass Through or Interference; or  
2.  No local limit exists, but the discharge did not change substantially in nature or constituents from the 
Industrial User's prior discharge when the BOPU was regularly in compliance with its WYPDES permit, 
and in the case of Interference, was in compliance with applicable sludge use or disposal requirements.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 
Article 10. - Miscellaneous provisions  
 
13.20.840 - Pretreatment charges and fees—BOPU fee schedule.  
 
A.  The BOPU may adopt reasonable fees for reimbursement of costs of setting up and operating the BOPU 
pretreatment program, which may include:  
1.  Fees for Individual Wastewater Discharge Permit applications including the cost of processing such 
applications;  
2.  Fees for monitoring, inspection, and surveillance procedures including the cost of collection and 
analyzing an Industrial User's discharge, and reviewing monitoring reports and certification statements 
submitted by Industrial Users;  
3.  Fees for reviewing and responding to accidental discharge procedures and construction;  
4.  Fees for filing appeals;  
5.  Fees to recover administrative and legal costs (not included in Section 13.20.840(A)(2)) of this Chapter 
associated with the enforcement activity taken by the control authority BOPU to address IU Industrial 
User’s noncompliance; and  
6.  Other fees as the BOPU may deem necessary to carry out the requirements contained herein. These fees 
relate solely to the matters covered by this Chapter and are separate from all other fees, fines, and 
penalties chargeable by the BOPU.  
(Ord. No. 4002, § 1, 10-28-13) 

 
 
75
 
Section 2. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its passage and publication.

Subject: A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts and Uses, 
amending the permissibility of various uses. 
Date: 
February 14, 2022  
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a UDC Text Amendment.   This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
•

February 14, 2022 
• 
Public Services Committee 
 
February 23, 2022 
• 
Second Reading consideration

Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their February 7, 2022, meeting and by unanimous vote, 
found that the item met the review criteria for approval and recommended the Governing Body approve 
the text amendment.  
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached.  The use table attached to the staff report has been 
removed since it is incorporated as Attachment A of the Ordinance under consideration.

PLANNING COMMISSION  
FEBRUARY 7, 2022 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, 
Districts and Uses, amending the permissibility of various uses. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-22-00001 
PREPARED BY: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts and Uses, amending the permissibility of 
various uses, as outlined in this staff report, noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012. The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant, and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The intent of this amendment is to modify the permissibility of specific uses in the UDC’s Use Table, 
Table 5-1, as well as delete repetitive uses.   Staff continues to evaluate the UDC while using it daily and 
recognize that more complex revisions in need of community input will be made in the future.    
 
DISCUSSION: 
 
Over recent months, it has come to the attention of staff that several of the defined terms of the UDC 
were repetitive, unnecessary, and posed barriers to certain uses.  Collaboratively, staff identified needed 
changes for the following uses: “Multi-dwelling Building (“Apartment”)”, “Live/Work”, “Mixed-use 
Dwelling”, “Urban Loft Building”, “Medical Care, Clinic”, “Medical Care, Office”, “Crematories”, “Railroad 
yards and maintenance buildings”, and “Nurseries, landscaping”.  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-22-00001 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
 
Staff has attached a copy of the Use Table which shows all proposed additions in red strikeout and 
additions as red underline.  Items marked with a “P” are permitted by right, an “A” require 
administrative approval by the Director, and a “C” require conditional use approval by the Board of 
Adjustment. No uses have been made more restrictive with this amendment.      
 
Summarized below are highlights of each use table item proposed for modification. 
 
• 
Multi-dwelling Building (“Apartment”)  
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a permitted use in the HR (High Density 
Residential) and NR-2 (Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density) zone districts. The intent 
behind this is to provide clarity that the specific use does not require conditional use approval; 
simply, one of the allowed lot types permitted require conditional use approval.  
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a conditional use in the CB (Community 
Business) zone district, allowing for introduction of multifamily development in areas commonly 
developed with regional retailers. As a conditional use, the Board of Adjustment would have to 
approve proposed projects to ensure multifamily development is appropriate for the specific 
location and that the development does not replace needed retail spaces. The use could 
complement existing retail and provide employee housing in CB zone districts.  
      
• 
Live/Work  
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a permitted use in the CB zone district, 
allowing for administrative introduction of a low-intensity residential use in commercial areas. 
Ultimately, this change would create the opportunity for businesses to incorporate a single 
residential unit in an individual building, provided it supports the commercial business within 
the building.   
  
• 
Mixed-use Dwelling 
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a permitted use in the CB zone district.  
Mixed-use buildings may be either small- or large-scale and are designed typically designed with 
ground floor retail or office.  This change would allow for introduction of mixed-use multifamily 
development in the CB zone district and encourage vertical mixed-use.  The use could 
complement existing retail and provide employee housing in CB zone districts.        
 
• 
Urban Loft Building  
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a conditional use in the CB zone district.  
Urban Loft Buildings are a residential use in a building designed for more than two principal 
dwelling units in an urban context. As a conditional use, the Board of Adjustment would have to 
approve proposed projects to ensure multifamily development is appropriate for the specific 
location and that the development does not replace needed retail spaces. The use could 
complement existing retail, provide employee housing in CB zone districts, and provide for 
urban-style infill projects in areas primarily developed in a suburban fashion.  
 
 

PLN-22-00001 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
• 
Medical Care, Clinic and Medical Care, Office  
 
This amendment would combine the two uses into one use within the use table.  They are 
essentially the same use, oftentimes are co-located, and are permissible in the same zone 
districts. The amendment would also allow these facilities as conditional uses in the MUR 
(Mixed-use Residential) zone districts to recognize existing centers that are classified as non-
conforming uses. Prior to UDC adoption in 2012, these centers were permitted in MUR districts.   
 
• 
Pet crematory  
 
This amendment would delete this use from the use table.  This is adequately covered under the 
“cemeteries” use (pet) and the “Service Business (Limited and General)” uses.  Staff is not 
certain why this use was initially singled out and limited to LI (Light Industrial) zone districts as a 
conditional use with the adoption of the UDC but believes deletion of this from the use table is 
acceptable. Presently, no businesses offering this service are located within an LI or SS (Support 
Services) zone district.     
  
• 
Railroad yards and maintenance buildings 
 
This would expand the use permissibility to allow it as a permitted use in the HI (Heavy 
Industrial) zone district.  Presently, this use is only allowed in the LI zone district. Allowing it in 
the HI zone district is logical, reflects existing conditions, and removes potential obstacles to 
future development.    
 
• 
Nurseries, landscaping  
 
This amendment would increase the amount of zone districts where landscape nurseries can be 
located. Presently, this use is only permissible as a conditional use in the AR (Agricultural 
Residential) zone district and permitted in the SS District.  This amendment would expand the 
allowance to allow them as principally permitted uses in the AG (Agriculture), AR, LI, and HI zone 
districts. The use would be permissible as a conditional use in the CB and MUB zone districts.   
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENT: 
 
The proposed amendments are reflected in Attachment A which is comprised of 3-pages. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 
2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
 
 

PLN-22-00001 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with several foundations and principles and policies 
within PlanCheyenne. Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and 
principles: 
 
Foundation 1 – Growing as a Community of Choice 
Synopsis: Cheyenne is a successful and livable community that provides excellent choices in 
housing and jobs. This Plan focuses on maintaining this high quality of life as our community 
grows and attracting new jobs so we can remain competitive in the region. The community 
will plan for and implement growth that provides opportunities for people of all 
generations, retains traits that have made it livable, and attract businesses and economic 
development. Modification of the use table, as proposed, provides support for existing 
businesses and clarifies where specific uses are permissible.    
 
Policy 1.4.d: Infill and Revitalization Incentives 
Synopsis: This policy directs the City to explore the use of incentives to encourage infill and 
revitalization activities in underutilized areas or areas that are simply in need of 
reinvestment. Increasing the flexibility in development regulations by adding uses to specific 
districts or expanding the area in which specific uses can locate, accomplishes this policy.   
 
Foundation 7 – Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area excels in providing services in a fiscally responsible way… The 
Plan aims to guide future growth to promote efficient use of public and private resources 
and to provide adequate public facilities to sustain our quality of life and to encourage 
continued growth. This includes revisiting permissible uses to adapt to changing trends and 
known deficiencies and to introduce housing options near employment opportunities.   
 
Principle 7.5:  Funding of Quality-of-Life Services 
Synopsis: Our community will consider how to fund quality of life services in an 
effective and efficient manner. 
 
The Design Principles for New Development  
Synopsis: This section of PlanCheyenne’s Community Plan provides guidance to ensure that 
new development is consistent with the community’s goals. These principles were originally 
established as part of the 2006 PlanCheyenne process to raise the bar for design in new 
development, help the Cheyenne Area retain its authenticity, and protect and enhance 
community character. To support their implementation, many of these principles were 
codified as part of the initial UDC. The plan further states that as the Cheyenne area 
continues to grow, the principles and supporting regulations should be applied consistently 
to new development to enhance community character and livability; should demonstrate 
the level of commitment and quality that the community desires; and should provide the 
predictability needed for property owners, businesses, and new residents to feel confident 
in their decision to invest in the community. Updates to the UDC are necessary to continue 
carrying out this vision as the community grows over time.     
 
The Design Principles to Guide New Development table supports multifamily development 
in the Community Business land use category and the other proposed changes to the use 
table are not in conflict, thus meeting this criterion.    
 

PLN-22-00001 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
As shown above, the proposed amendment meets several of the Foundation, Policy, and 
Principles of PlanCheyenne and satisfies this review criterion.  The proposed amendment is in 
accordance with the general goals and consistent with PlanCheyenne principles and policy. 
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal 
Planning and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State 
Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(A) Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other 
structures;  
(B) Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C) Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D) Density of population; and 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence, or other purposes. 
 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
updating lot coverage area and open space; thus, fulfilling requirements 
of this statute.  
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
 
(i) 
In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
(A) Lessen congestion in the streets; 
(B) Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
(C) Promote health and general welfare; 
(D) Provide adequate light and air; 
(E) Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
(F) Avoid undue concentration of population;  and 
(G) Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, 
sewerage, schools, parks and other public requirements. 
(ii) 
With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the 
character of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular 
uses; 
(iii) 
With a view to conserving the value of buildings and 
encouraging the most appropriate use of land throughout the 
city or town; and  
(iv) 
With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain 
neighborhoods or districts and a view to preserving, 
rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and 
encouraging compatible uses within the neighborhoods or 
districts, but no regulation made to carry out the purposes of 
this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an 
unconstitutional taking without compensation. 

PLN-22-00001 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
updating the use table as to the permissibility of specific uses. 
Regulations of this nature are allowable under state statue and may be 
modified by the Governing Body.    
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how 
regulations regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The 
Governing Body possesses the authority to amend regulations and 
therefore, can amend the UDC to reflect this text amendment. 
 
As listed above, the proposed amendment satisfies this review criterion. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne and satisfies this review criterion. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. Amending the UDC 
in this fashion provides more flexibility with specific uses. The amendments accomplish several policies 
within PlanCheyenne and are enabled by Wyoming State Zoning statutes. Lastly, updating this will not 
jeopardize the overall health, safety, and welfare of the community. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to the Unified 
Development Code to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts and Uses, amending 
the permissibility of various uses as outlined in this staff report, noting the request meets the review 
criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
ATTACHMENTS:   
 
1. Use Table With Proposed Changes REMOVED FROM GOVERNING BODY PACKET, SEE ORDINANCE 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) TO CREATE A COMMON LANDSCAPING PLAN AND 
DEFINITION OF DEVELOPMENT FOOTPRINT.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to create a common landscaping plan 
and definition of development footprint. 
 
Section 3. That on February 26, 2022, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment with modifications. 
 
Section 4. That Section 1.4.3 of the UDC is amended to add the following definition:  
 
“Development Footprint. The development footprint is the total land area that is 
permanently affected by development activities, and includes buildings, structures, 
hardscape, utilities, roads and parking areas, maneuvering areas, storage areas, areas 
associated with the use on the property, and areas cleared of natural vegetation and topsoil.”  
 
Section 5. That Section 6.3.10, is created to read as follows:  
 
“6.3.10 Common Landscape Plan 
As an alternative to individual site landscaping, developments of one hundred acres or 
larger in Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning districts may submit a 
common landscape plan that would contribute to uniformity of a development’s collective 
appearance. Common landscape plans shall focus on providing landscaping that is 
beneficial to the public, consistent with the general landscape character and context of the 
area, and water conservation. Common landscape plans shall be administratively approved 
by the Director. 
 
a. Submittal Requirements.  Applications for a common landscape plan may be 
initiated by all property owner(s) of all property subject to the common 
landscape plan, or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through 
mutual agreement or a property owner’s association. 
 
b. Application, Submittal, and Phasing. An applicant shall submit a conceptual 
common landscape plan, prepared by a licensed landscape architect, for the 
entire development based on presumed development footprints for the plan 
area. Installation of landscaping may be developed in phases and the developer 
By Stefanie Boster at 8:57 pm, Feb 08, 2022

 
 
2 
 
shall submit phasing plan in conjunction with submittal of common landscape 
plan.  Phasing plans shall show general locations of landscaping, materials, 
plantings, groundcover, anticipated development footprints, and any transferred 
points within the plan area.  As development occurs, site / landscape plans shall 
be submitted and reviewed by the City to determine that each site meets the 
intent of the common landscape plan. 
 
c. Common Landscape Plan Standards.  The landscape standards of Chapter 6.3 
of the UDC shall apply, except as set forth below.  
 
1. Required Internal Landscape Area. The Required Internal Landscape Area 
of Section 6.3.3 shall be ten (10) percent of the development footprint. 
Required internal landscaping may be transferred to other areas within the 
area subject to the plan. The use of required internal landscape trees within 
and adjacent to parking areas and as an addition to required street trees, is 
encouraged.  
2. Street Trees. The Street Tree standards of 6.3.6 shall be adjacent to 
arterials, collectors, local streets, and interstates subject to City Forestry’s 
standards for street trees and the Street Design Standards of Section 4.3, at 
the rate of one tree per sixty (60) feet of street frontage.  Street trees shall 
be in the adjacent public right-of-way or seventy-five (75) feet of the 
property line adjacent to a street right-of-way if circumstances unique to 
the site prevent the placement of street trees within the right-of-way. 
 
d. Tree Reduction and Relief of Requirements Allowance. Up to a twenty (20) 
percent reduction of the required number of internal and/or street trees may be 
granted by the Director based on specific site conditions and to best meet the 
development’s collective appearance. If a tree reduction is approved: 
 
1. A per tree monetary donation shall be made by the applicant to the City, for 
every tree reduced, to be deposited in a specific fund for use in purchasing 
and installing trees for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway and other City 
lands, including but not limited to, open spaces, park areas and rights-of-
way. 
2. The City Forester shall determine the monetary donation per tree yearly on 
January 1st of each year, based on the average wholesale and installation 
cost of trees from three regional wholesale nurseries. This fee shall be in 
effect for the calendar year and be available to the public. 
3. A per tree monetary reduction can count toward the point system as a new 
tree. 
 

 
 
3 
 
e. 
Review Criteria. In reviewing, providing comments, and making decisions on 
a proposed common landscape plan, the Director shall use the following 
criteria: 
 
1. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves the 
goals and policies of PlanCheyenne to the same or better degree than the 
subject standard; and results in benefits to the community that are equivalent 
to or better than compliance with the subject standard; or 
2. The required landscaping on each lot is not practical because of location, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can 
accommodate site-specific issues. 
3. The plan represents a logical phasing plan for the development.   
4. Adequate utilities exist to serve the landscape elements detailed in the plan. 
5. The plan shall not place an undue fiscal burden on the City or other public 
entities.   
6. An entity has been identified for maintenance of common landscape plan 
elements and any necessary agreements can be executed within sixty (60) 
days of approval. 
7. Incorporates an area of at least forty (40) contiguous acres.  For the purposes 
of area calculation, intervening rights-of-way shall be included in the 
measurement.   
8. The plan is prepared by a licensed landscape architect. 
 
f. Amendments and Alterations. Amendments to the common landscape plan 
may only be initiated with the consent of all property owner(s) of all property 
subject to the common landscape plan or an agent or entity authorized by all 
property owner(s) through mutual agreement or a property owners’ association.  
g. Recording and Approval. Following approval of a Common Landscape Plan, 
the Director shall sign the plan and the applicant shall then record the plan on 
each affected lot or parcel in the office of the County Clerk and be responsible 
for any associated fees. The applicant shall provide a copy of the recorded plan 
to the Director prior to issuance of any building permits. 
 
h. Abandonment of Plan. In the event the applicant wishes to abandon the 
approved Common Landscape Plan, the Director must determine that 
abandonment of the plan is not detrimental to the plan area and that the 
applicant has taken reasonable and necessary steps to mitigate any non-
conforming site conditions on existing sites in the plan area. Requests for 
abandonment may be initiated by the Governing Body, the Director, by all 
property owner(s) of all property subject to the common landscape plan, or an 

 
 
4 
 
agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through mutual agreement 
or a property owner’s association. Upon Director determination that 
abandonment is acceptable, the Director shall sign any necessary releases to be 
filed with the office of the County Clerk.”  
 
Section 6. The aforementioned sections of the UDC are hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
FEBRUARY 7, 2022 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to create a common landscaping plan and definition of 
development footprint. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-21-00017 
PREPARED BY: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to create a common landscaping process and a definition of development footprint as outlined in this 
staff report, noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012. The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant, and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The intent of this amendment is to create flexibility in landscaping options to allow an administrative 
option for a common landscape plan for Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning district 
developments that are over 100-acres.   The goal of the common landscape plan is to create overall 
plans that would contribute to uniformity of a development’s collective appearance, place landscaping 
in areas that are more beneficial to the public, and promote water conservation.  
 
DISCUSSION: 
 
City staff has been working together with Parks and Greenway Planning and the City Forester to allow 
for alternative landscape requirements in areas where strict adherence to the landscape ordinance may 
not be in the best interest of the community.  Specifically, issues with our current landscape code have 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
arisen in large development projects, such as Bison Business Park and Cheyenne Business Park, where 
alternative landscape plans could result in higher quality landscaping that is contextually more 
appropriate for the character of the area.  Areas where staff envisions the proposed Common Landscape 
Plan option include large industrial developments in industrial zone districts that are presently 
developing.  This includes business parks such as Bison Business Park, lands east of the Campstool Road, 
and future business parks with property owners’ associations that may wish to annex. Under current 
landscaping standards large lots are required to calculate landscape requirements on the entirety of the 
site and administrative flexibility is limited to 10%, individual buffer yards, and individual sites.   
 
Summarized below are highlights of the proposed amendment. 
 
• 
A new definition, “Development Footprint,” is proposed to allow developments to utilize 
their disturbed area for the purpose of calculating internal landscaping points and tree 
requirements, instead of the entirety of the lot.   
• 
The Common Landscape Plan will only apply to developments comprised of 100-acres or 
more that are zoned Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning district.   
• 
Common Landscape Plans may be initiated by all landowners or an organization authorized 
to act on their behalf, like a property owners’ association. Approved conceptual plans are 
recorded with County Real Estate Records on the individual properties.  
• 
Common Landscape Plans are required to be prepared by a licensed landscape architect. 
Plans may estimate future development footprints and revise them as necessary.  
Subsequent plans for an individual site may be prepared by a design professional, but are 
required to comply with the approved concept plan.    
• 
The internal landscape point calculations are based on the development footprint, instead 
of the entire lot area.  The calculation is based on a 90% impervious coverage of the 
development footprint.  Current LI levels are 85% of the entire area, and HI are 90%. 
• 
A Common Landscape Plan allows for transfer of landscaping elements to other areas of the 
plan. The process encourages transferred elements to be located along public rights-of-way 
or parking areas. Transfers are required to be identified in the plan.   
• 
The street tree standards of UDC 6.3.6 are modified to require street trees at the rate of one 
tree per 60 linear feet of street frontage instead of one per 40 feet. The street trees, as well 
as any transferred landscaping points may be clustered and are required to be located 
within 75’ of the property line adjacent to the right-of-way to allow for more creative design 
that may better harmonize with the surrounding area.   
• 
The ability to seek administrative relief of tree planting requirements is allowed. This could 
include up to a 20% reduction of the required internal or street trees. Approval is based on 
specific site conditions and to ensure that the reduction does not negatively affect the 
development’s collective appearance. If a tree reduction were to be approved, the applicant 
would be required to pay a cash in-lieu fee equal to the cost of the tree and installation.  In-
lieu fees would be deposited into a specific fund for use in purchasing trees for the Greater 
Cheyenne Greenway and other City lands. The in-lieu fee is presently calculated annually by 
the City Forester for similar relief of street tree requirements.  
• 
Specific review criteria are incorporated to aide in the administrative decision-making 
process.  Key components of the review criteria include demonstration that adequate 
infrastructure exists to serve the plan area, the proposed plan does not put an undue 
burden on the City or other public utilities, and that an entity responsible for ensuring 
maintenance, such as a property owners’ association, has been identified.    
• 
Provisions for plan modification and plan abandonment administratively are included.   
 
 
 

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
PROPOSED AMENDMENT: 
 
The proposed amendment creates a new definition in the definitions section of Article 1 and a new 
section within the Landscaping section of Article 6.  Upon recommendation by the Planning Commission, 
an ordinance reflecting the amendments as recommended will be drafted and presented to the 
Governing Body for approval.  The proposed amendments are shown below.       
 
New definition in UDC Section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, which reads as follows:  
 
“Development Footprint. The development footprint is the total land area that is permanently 
affected by development activities, and includes buildings, structures, hardscape, utilities, roads 
and parking areas, maneuvering areas, storage areas, areas associated with the use on the 
property, and areas cleared of natural vegetation and topsoil.” 
 
Create UDC Section 6.3.10 which reads as follows:  
 
“6.3.10 Common Landscape Plan 
As an alternative to individual site landscaping, developments of one hundred acres or larger in 
Light Industrial (LI) and Heavy Industrial (HI) zoning districts may submit a common landscape 
plan that would contribute to uniformity of a development’s collective appearance. Common 
landscape plans shall focus on providing landscaping that is beneficial to the public, consistent 
with the general landscape character and context of the area, and water conservation. Common 
landscape plans shall be administratively approved by the Director. 
 
a. Submittal Requirements.  Applications for a common landscape plan may be 
initiated by all property owner(s) of all property subject to the common landscape 
plan, or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through mutual 
agreement or a property owner’s association. 
 
b. Application, Submittal, and Phasing. An applicant shall submit a conceptual 
common landscape plan, prepared by a licensed landscape architect, for the entire 
development based on presumed development footprints for the plan area. 
Installation of landscaping may be developed in phases and the developer shall 
submit phasing plan in conjunction with submittal of common landscape plan.  
Phasing plans shall show general locations of landscaping, materials, plantings, 
groundcover, anticipated development footprints, and any transferred points within 
the plan area.  As development occurs, site / landscape plans shall be submitted and 
reviewed by the City to determine that each site meets the intent of the common 
landscape plan. 
 
c. Common Landscape Plan Standards.  The landscape standards of Chapter 6.3 of the 
UDC shall apply, except as set forth below.  
 
1. Required Internal Landscape Area. The Required Internal Landscape Area of Section 6.3.3 
shall be ten (10) percent of the development footprint. Required internal landscaping may 
be transferred to other areas within the area subject to the plan. The use of required 
internal landscape trees within and adjacent to parking areas and as an addition to required 
street trees, is encouraged.  
2. 
Street Trees. The Street Tree standards of 6.3.6 shall be adjacent to 
arterials, collectors, local streets, and interstates subject to City 

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
Forestry’s standards for street trees and the Street Design Standards of 
Section 4.3, at the rate of one tree per sixty (60) feet of street frontage.  
Street trees shall be in the adjacent public right-of-way or seventy-five 
(75) feet of the property line adjacent to a street right-of-way if 
circumstances unique to the site prevent the placement of street trees 
within the right-of-way. 
 
d. Tree Reduction and Relief of Requirements Allowance. Up to a twenty (20) percent 
reduction of the required number of internal and/or street trees may be granted by 
the Director based on specific site conditions and to best meet the development’s 
collective appearance. If a tree reduction is approved: 
1. A per tree monetary donation shall be made by the applicant to the City, for 
every tree reduced, to be deposited in a specific fund for use in purchasing and 
installing trees for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway and other City lands, 
including but not limited to, open spaces, park areas and rights-of-way. 
2. The City Forester shall determine the monetary donation per tree yearly on 
January 1st of each year, based on the average wholesale and installation cost 
of trees from three regional wholesale nurseries. This fee shall be in effect for 
the calendar year and be available to the public. 
3. A per tree monetary reduction can count toward the point system as a new 
tree. 
 
e. Review Criteria. In reviewing, providing comments, and making decisions on a proposed 
common landscape plan, the Director shall use the following criteria: 
1. The proposed alternative design achieves the intent of the subject design 
standard to the same or better degree than the subject standard; achieves the 
goals and policies of PlanCheyenne to the same or better degree than the 
subject standard; and results in benefits to the community that are equivalent 
to or better than compliance with the subject standard; or 
2. The required landscaping on each lot is not practical because of location, 
topographic, or site constraints while the alternative design can accommodate 
site-specific issues. 
3. The plan represents a logical phasing plan for the development.   
4. Adequate utilities exist to serve the landscape elements detailed in the plan. 
5. The plan shall not place an undue fiscal burden on the City or other public 
entities.   
6. An entity has been identified for maintenance of common landscape plan 
elements and any necessary agreements can be executed within sixty (60) days 
of approval. 
7. Incorporates an area of at least one hundred (100) acres.  For the purposes of 
area calculation, intervening rights-of-way shall be included in the 
measurement.   
8. The plan is prepared by a licensed landscape architect. 
 
f. Amendments and Alterations. Amendments to the common landscape plan may only 
be initiated with the consent of all property owner(s) of all property subject to the 
common landscape plan or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) 
through mutual agreement or a property owners’ association.  

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
 
g. Recording and Approval. Following approval of a Common Landscape Plan, the Director 
shall sign the plan and the applicant shall then record the plan on each affected lot or 
parcel in the office of the County Clerk and be responsible for any associated fees. The 
applicant shall provide a copy of the recorded plan to the Director prior to issuance of 
any building permits. 
 
h. Abandonment of Plan. In the event the applicant wishes to abandon the approved 
Common Landscape Plan, the Director must determine that abandonment of the plan is 
not detrimental to the plan area and that the applicant has taken reasonable and 
necessary steps to mitigate any non-conforming site conditions on existing sites in the 
plan area. Requests for abandonment may be initiated by the Governing Body, the 
Director, by all property owner(s) of all property subject to the common landscape plan, 
or an agent or entity authorized by all property owner(s) through mutual agreement or 
a property owner’s association. Upon Director determination that abandonment is 
acceptable, the Director shall sign any necessary releases to be filed with the office of 
the County Clerk.”  
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 
2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with Foundation 7, and principles and policies within 
PlanCheyenne. Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and 
principles: 
 
Foundation 7 – Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area excels in providing services in a fiscally responsible way…  The 
Plan aims to guide future growth to promote efficient use of public and private resources 
and to provide adequate public facilities to sustain our quality of life and to encourage 
continued growth. This includes allowing flexibility with site development for large sites and 
business parks.   
 
Principle 7.2: Adequate Public Services 
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area will provide adequate public facilities and services for 
current and future residents in a fiscally responsible manner. The developments commonly 
eligible for this process are typically located on the fringe and appropriate development 
could reduce the necessity for water positively affecting the capacity of the water system. 
 
Policy 7.2.C: Maintain Level of Service  
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area will coordinate with service providers and other governmental 
organizations that provide services to community residents to ensure that existing and new 
neighborhoods have adequate services, and that existing public facilities are properly 
maintained to serve the needs of current and future residents.  

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
 
Principle 7.4: Provision of Essential Services 
Synopsis: Our community will continue to provide essential services in an effective and 
efficient manner. 
 
Policy 7.4.B: Long-Term Water Supply 
Synopsis: Continue to coordinate with the Board of Public Utilities to plan for a long-term 
water supply, including conservation measures. 
 
Principle 7.5:  Funding of Quality-of-Life Services 
Synopsis: Our community will consider how to fund quality of life services in an 
effective and efficient manner. 
 
Policy 7.5.A: Funding for Quality-of-Life Services 
Synopsis: Our community will continue to identify and provide funding for services and 
amenities that contribute to quality of life and economic vitality of the community, including 
parks and recreation, cultural and library facilities, and other services. 
 
As shown above, the proposed amendment meets several of the Foundation, Policy, and 
Principles of PlanCheyenne and satisfies this review criterion.      
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal 
Planning and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State 
Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(A) Height, number of stories and size of buildings and other 
structures;  
(B) Percentage of lot that may be occupied; 
(C) Size of yards, courts and other open spaces; 
(D) Density of population; and 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence, or other purposes. 
 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
updating lot coverage area and open space; thus, fulfilling requirements 
of this statute.  
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
 
(i) 
In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
(A) Lessen congestion in the streets; 
(B) Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
(C) Promote health and general welfare; 
(D) Provide adequate light and air; 
(E) Prevent the overcrowding of land; 

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
(F) Avoid undue concentration of population;  and 
(G) Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, water, 
sewerage, schools, parks and other public requirements. 
(ii) 
With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the 
character of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular 
uses; 
(iii) 
With a view to conserving the value of buildings and 
encouraging the most appropriate use of land throughout the 
city or town; and  
(iv) 
With consideration given to the historic integrity of certain 
neighborhoods or districts and a view to preserving, 
rehabilitating and maintaining historic properties and 
encouraging compatible uses within the neighborhoods or 
districts, but no regulation made to carry out the purposes of 
this paragraph is valid to the extent it constitutes an 
unconstitutional taking without compensation. 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
encouraging landscaping better suited  for the large, industrial 
development. Regulations of this nature are allowable under state 
statue and may be modified by the Governing Body.    
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how 
regulations regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The 
Governing Body possesses the authority to amend regulations and 
therefore can amend the UDC to reflect this text amendment. 
 
As listed above, the proposed amendment satisfies this review criterion. 
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne and satisfies this review criterion. 
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. Amending the UDC 
in this fashion provides a development option to create landscapes that are beneficial to the public, 
consistent with the general landscape character and context of a specific area, and promote water 
conservation. The amendments accomplish several policies within PlanCheyenne and are enabled by 
Wyoming State Zoning statutes. Lastly, updating this will not jeopardize the overall health, safety, and 
welfare of the community. 
 
 

PLN-21-00017 
 
 
February 7, 2022 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code to create a common 
landscaping plan process and a definition of development footprint as outlined in this staff report, 
noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 


--- END OF FILE ---

(g)

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00004: Annexation of 521 Southwest Drive
REQUEST:
An annexation of 48.6 acres of land
LOCATION:
East of Southwest Drive, south of I-80
APPLICANT:
Steil Surveying Services, LLC
Casey Palma
AGENT:
Steil Surveying Services, LLC
Casey Palma
OWNER:
Tru Grit Land Development LLC
PURPOSE:
To allow future development
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 48.6 acres of land for future development  most likely residential. 
The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as a portion of a platted  lot, one ownership parcel. 
The land is currently developed in the County with one single-family home. A zone change is proposed 
concurrently with this annexation. Both applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the 
Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County MU Mixed Use to the City AG Agricultural zone. The purpose of this 
annexation is to allow future urban development 
 future zone change and 
platting action is anticipated to create urban development patterns. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned MU Mixed Use in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the City AG Agricultural zone. The City AG zone is intended for low-intensity 
operations, including agriculture, and is appropriate as a holding zone until such time urban 
development patterns are finalized. Future development is anticipated to be largely residential with 
possibly a mix of commercial uses (according to the comprehensive plan designation for the area). 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-use Residential 
County MU 
Rural Residential

Mixed-use Residential

Residential 

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be 
connected to City sanitary sewer instead of having septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. For all intents, the property is surrounded by the City (the only non-annexed area is 
the railroad right-of-way). The contiguity on three sides and the proximity to surrounding urban-
style development makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of 
this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and 
economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City sewer lines exist in the vicinity and both water and 
sewer lines would likely be extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 75.1% contiguous, along all but the eastern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 

UDC-22-00004
February 14, 2022
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on January 14, 2022. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for February 14, 2022. Twenty (20) business days 
prior to the announced public hearing is January 14, 2022. The earliest confirmation of delivery 
was dated January 18, 2022. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage the maximization of existing infrastructure and the reduction of 
County pockets. This property is near existing urban infrastructure (although water lines would require 
significant extension), and the annexation would effectively eliminate a County pocket. 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with a mix of urban residential and lower-intensity commercial uses. These areas 
are expected to develop with full access to urban services  such areas are best served by being within 
City limits. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
New septic systems will not be allowed. 
- 
Water and sewer are not immediately adjacent to this parcel and extensions will be needed 
with development. 
- 
Annexation will require future development to comply with City-adopted regulations 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map 
4. Supporting photos

of-way marker; thence continuing along said north line, along a curve, concave to the southeast, 
with a radius of 5455.39 feet, curve delta 16 deg 27' 00", arc length of 1566.28’ and chord bearing 
of N.81 deg 17' 58"E., a distance of 1560.91’ to the POB.
is hereby changed to AG Agricultural.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00004-01: 521 Southwest Drive Zone Change
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 48.6 acres of land from County MU 
Mixed Use to AG Agricultural   
LOCATION:
East of Southwest Drive, South of I-80
OWNER:
Tru Grit Land Development LLC
ZONING:
County MU Mixed Use
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Residential
PURPOSE:
To give land a City zone in concert with annexation
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tract 1 (less 22.38 acres), H and B Tracts, Laramie County, Wyoming to 
AG Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval, with the following condition: 
1. 
change map.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural 
for 48.6 acres. The land is undeveloped. The impetus behind the proposed zone change is to give land a City 
zone in concert with an annexation.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary and some existing 
infrastructure exists to the west. There are also opportunities to tie into infrastructure. 
Development will require infrastructure extensions and improvements.   
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-use Residential 
County MU 
Rural Residential

Mixed-use Residential

Mixed-use Residential

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Residential on the Future Land 
Use Map. The optimal zoning for the proposed future land use designation is MUR Mixed-use 
Residential, although a variety of residential and lower-intensity commercial zones may also be 
appropriate. 
 
While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the land uses identified by the Future Land 
Use Map, the AG zone is 
finalized, and such zoning would not have an adverse effect on the potential for the property to 
develop in accordance with the plans and policies of the comprehensive plan in the future.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The existing zoning to the southwest is AR, while to the east, west, and north there is LI zoning, 
and to the south is MUB zoning. There is also PUD zoning to the west. The property to the west 
zoned LI is the animal shelter. Properties zoned LI to the east are across railroad tracks, and the 
area zoned LI to the north is undeveloped. The areas zoned PUD and MUB are also 
undeveloped. The AG zone is compatible with the areas zoned AR, and, as a holding zone, is 
compatible with the other undeveloped areas surrounding it.  
 
The future land uses anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan for this area anticipate higher-
intensity, urban-style uses. While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the Future Land 
development occurs. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County MU zone allows a wide variety of uses; however, most of those uses would 
require water and sewer service, and the South Cheyenne District is unable to service this 
parcel. Therefore annexation to the City and the establishment of City zoning would be required 
to establish the types of uses typically considered by the current County zone. While AG zoning 
within the City only allows low density residential and lower-intensity agricultural uses, it is also 
appropriate as a holding zone until more urban-style development is ready to occur. 
    
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 have limited applicability to the proposed AG zone, as AG zoned land 
is highly limited in allowed uses and is intended to have limited infrastructure improvements, if 

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
any. By zoning the land as AG, the property would likely remain undeveloped until future zoning, 
subdivision, and other development actions occur. The standards of Article 4 would be required 
to be met with those future development actions.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The AG zone is intended to develop with primarily agricultural uses or to leave land in a natural 
state. This zone prohibits most of the typical urban-style uses.  The AG zone is applicable to 
areas where significant natural lands or open spaces are to be protected, or areas designated 
Agriculture/Rural or Rural Residential by the Future Land Use Map. The AG zone is also 
applicable as an interim or holding zone for areas that may be further developed in the long 
term, but where minimal development activity in the interim is acceptable and will not 
prematurely establish development patterns. 
 
This property is anticipated to develop with urban-style uses in the future. However, the AG 
zone is appliable as a holding zone and is intended to leave the land in an undeveloped state  
which would allow future rezoning and development of the property when development is 
appropriate. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. While the 
subject property is anticipated to develop with urban-style uses in the future, establishing 
specific development patterns at this time may be premature. Interim agricultural style uses or 
very-low density residential uses may be appropriate until such time as the land is ready for 
urban-style development. 
  
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00004-01
February 7, 2022
8)
Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the clarity of the application. The 
application, the neighbor letter, and discussions with the applicant all point to a request for AG zoning; 
. The map should be corrected for clarity. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation. While the property is anticipated to 
develop with urban-style uses in the future, the AG zone is applicable as a holding zone. Staff 
recommends approval of the zoning map amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County MU to AG. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO HI HEAVY INDUSTRIAL FOR 
LAND LOCATED NORTH OF CAMPSTOOL ROAD, WEST OF 
PROGRESS CIRCLE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1,
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on February 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Tracts 20-22, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Addition Third Filing; and Lots 1-2, 
Block 1, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Fifth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to
HI Heavy Industrial.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as 
described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.
