STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00005: Rocky Mountain Industrial Park Zone Change 
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 23.4 acres of land from CB 
Community Business to HI Heavy Industrial   
LOCATION:
North of Campstool, west of Progress Circle
OWNER:
Cheyenne Leads
ZONING:
CB Community Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Industrial
PURPOSE:
To allow outdoor storage use
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tracts 20-22, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park, 3rd Filing; and Lots 1-2, 
Block 1, Rocky Mountain Industrial Park, 5th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming to HI Heavy Industrial as shown 
in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for 23.4 acres. The land is undeveloped. The impetus behind the proposed zone change is to allow 
the industrial use of Outdoor Storage on at least one of the subject parcels.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development.   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Industrial, and the zoning classification of 
HI is one of the optimal zones for areas so designated. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Industrial  
CB (Community Business) 
Undeveloped 
North 
Urban Residential 
County A-2, County HI 
Undeveloped 
South

Limited Industrial 
(warehousing)

Industrial 
CB 
Office, Limited Industrial 
(warehousing) 
West 
Urban Residential, Industrial 
County HI, HI 
Undeveloped 

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage industrial areas to be located outside of activity centers 
and with ready access to regional transportation routes. This area supports these objectives. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use designation of the subject property, HI zoning is 
appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Nearby property to the north and west are largely undeveloped and across a railroad track. 
Property to the south is zoned HI and developed with a large-scale distribution facility. Property 
to the east is zoned CB and contains both offices and warehousing-type facilities. The 
development patterns in this area are compatible with the proposed HI zoning. While CB allows 
more commercial-style development, the CB region to the east is surrounded by HI zoning. HI 
zoned land is required to have buffers to areas zoned CB.  
 
The comprehensive plan encourages industrial uses and zoning for all of the property between 
the railroad and I-80 in this area of the community. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is currently zoned CB Community Business. CB zoning requires building design 
standards and is intended for regional-serving commercial and service uses. The proposed 
zoning of HI has no design standards and allows more intense uses which are intended to be 
buffered from the standard residential/commercial areas of the community. While the property 
has the ability to develop according to the standards of either zone district, the subject property 
is more likely to develop with industrial-style uses than with commercial service style uses. A HI 
zone is required to provide a buffer to CB zoned areas, but the parcels in question should have 
enough area to be able to provide the intended buffer. 
 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 regarding block size and connections are not really applicable to 
industrial areas; and due to the adjacent railroad and highway, north-south options for 
connections are very limited. The area is already platted and no replatting actions are currently 
proposed with this zone change. The existing subdivision pattern better matches HI zone 
expectations than CB zone expectations.  

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The HI zone is intended for uses of very high intensity which should be able to be isolated and 
buffered from other uses. This zoning is most appropriate in areas with access to major regional 
transportation infrastructure and areas designated Industrial in the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
While HI zoning does not have design standards, other development standards are still 
applicable. The property zoned HI should be able to provide the required landscape buffer to 
the adjacent CB zoning, and certain HI uses are required to have buffers from streets. There 
have been no official applications submitted in concert with this zone change application, and HI 
zoning will allow a variety of more intense uses  should the potential development action that 
prompted this zone change not be executed, the HI zone is appropriate at this location. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No recommendations were received through the review process.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00005
February 7, 2022
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received suggesting the proposed zoning was compatible 
with the comprehensive plan. Comments were also received noting that a buffer would be required to 
the adjacent CB zoned parcels. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The HI zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use designation for the subject property and surrounding 
development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant  (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from CB to HI. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_75873550.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE CREATING A NEW SECTION 9.08.120,  MALICIOUS 
HARASSMENT BASED ON RACE, COLOR, RELIGION, ANCESTRY, 
SEX, SEXUAL ORIENTATION, GENDER IDENTITY AND EXPRESSION, 
NATIONAL ORIGIN, OR DISABILITY, OF CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES 
AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC 
PEACE AND WELFARE, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING.” 
 
 
BE IT HEREBY ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That a new Section 9.08.120, malicious harassment based on race, color, 
religion, ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity and expression, national origin, or 
disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace 
and Welfare, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created as set out in this 
ordinance. 
 
9.08.120 
Malicious harassment based on race, color, religion, ancestry, sex, sexual 
orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, or disability. 
 
A. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with the 
specific intent to intimidate or harass another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, or disability, 
commit any of the following acts: 
 
1. 
Assault, batter, assault and batter of another person; 
 
2. 
Damage, destroy, vandalize, deface, trespass upon, or steal any real or 
personal property of another person; or 
 
3. 
Threaten, by word or act, to do any act prohibited by subparagraph 1. or 2. 
of this subsection if there is reasonable cause to believe that such act will occur. 
 
 
B. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with specific 
intent to incite or produce, and which is likely to incite or produce, imminent violence, which 
violence would be directed against another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, or disability, 
make or transmit, or cause or allow to be made or transmitted, any telephonic, computerized, or 
electronic message. 
 
 
C. 
It shall be unlawful and an offense for any person to maliciously and with specific 
intent to incite or produce, and which is likely to incite or produce, imminent violence, which 
violence would be directed against another person because of that person’s race, color, religion, 
ancestry, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, national origin, or disability, 
broadcast, publish, or distribute, or cause or allow to be broadcast, published, or distributed, any 
message or material. 
By Stefanie Boster at 6:01 pm, Feb 10, 2022

 
 
2 
 
 
D. 
Any person violating any provision of subsection A., B. or C. of this section shall 
be guilty of a misdemeanor on a first offense and, upon conviction, shall be punished pursuant to 
the general provisions of Section 1.24.010 of the city code, excluding costs, fees, and assessments, 
or by both such fine and imprisonment. 
 
 
Section 2.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon passage and 
publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_37282913.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
January 18, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Interfund Loan Resolution for 6th Penny Projects 
 
 
On January 24, 2022, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
for consideration of an interfund loan from the One Percent Sales Tax Fund (5th Penny) to the 
Specific Purpose Option Sales Tax Fund (6th Penny).   
The City will begin receiving its share of sales tax from the recently approved 6th penny ballot this 
coming June.  I have estimated, based on current sales tax collections, that this tax will be fully 
collected by early fall of 2026, or a little over four years.  However, it may be faster or slower, 
based on the economy and how much sales tax we receive each month.   
State law permits municipalities to loan money from one fund to another with approval of the 
Governing Body.  Therefore, I have conducted a cash flow analysis for the City’s $62 million in 
6th penny projects and determined that it is beneficial to borrow excess 5th penny funds to complete 
the 6th penny projects that are “shovel ready” rather than waiting until sales tax collections are 
sufficient.  This will help mitigate inflationary risk that most likely will occur if the projects are 
delayed.  Borrowing funds internally will also alleviate the need to obtain more costly external 
financing from a bank or other lending institution. 
The $42.5 million 2019-2022 5th penny ballot, which is collected over a four-year period, was fully 
collected in only 31 months, which leaves 17 months of sales tax collections through December 
2022 to be considered “overages”.  If collections remain strong, I have estimated total overages 
could be nearly $30 million.  This fund currently has over $52.5 million in cash and is earning 
approximately 1% in interest.   
Therefore, my recommendation to the Governing Body is to approve this interfund loan in an 
amount not to exceed $14.89 million with an interest rate of 1% over a four-year term for the three 
6th penny projects that are ready and have budgeted for interest costs, including construction of the 
three fire stations, purchasing fire trucks and replacement of the cemetery irrigation system. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.  




--- END OF FILE ---

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
01/18/2022 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PLN-21-00016:  Future Land Use Plan Amendment from CB to MU-C for 
Logan Ave at E. Lincolnway 
REQUEST: 
A request to amend the Future Land Use Plan from CB (Community 
Business) District to Mixed-Use Commercial (Mixed-Use Commercial) 
LOCATION: 
West of Campbell Avenue, South of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway, and 
North of Nationway 
APPLICANT: 
Jamie Floyd 
OWNER: 
Various 
ZONING: 
CB (Community Business) and MUB (Mixed-Use Business Emphasis) District 
PURPOSE: 
To account for the urban and residential nature of the neighborhood. 
PREPARED BY: 
Mark Christensen, AICP Planner II 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-21-00016 
 
 
January 18, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan for property generally located west 
of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway from CB 
(Community Business) to MU-C (Mixed-Use Commercial), noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
PlanCheyenne (2016 Community Plan) 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting amending the Future Land Use Map for .10 acres of land from Community 
Business to Mixed-Use Commercial to accommodate a zone change to permit a pet grooming facility. Upon 
receiving the application for the Future Land Use Amendment, staff determined the amendment should be 
expanded to account for the urban and residential nature of the surrounding neighborhood. The expanded 
amendment encompasses 66 parcels. 
 
A concurrent application for a zoning map amendment has been submitted. The zoning map amendment will 
accompany the future land use amendment at City Council. If the application for the zoning map amendment 
is approved, the applicant will be required to submit a site plan application for a change of use. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is shown as Community Business in the Future Land Use Plan.  The Community Business 
category emphasizes a range of retail and office uses to serve neighborhoods and the community and 
region. The Community Business designation and supports the CB (Community Business) Zone District. 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

PLN-21-00016 
 
 
January 18, 2022 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Map of the 
Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following considerations outlined in the 2014 
Community Plan (Build Chapter page 3-3).  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the community 
or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.    
 
The Community Business future land use designation does not match the character of the 
subject properties. Many of the properties within the future land use amendment are 
residential. To expand a residence or add more residential units to the area, an applicant would 
be required to rezone to a district that permits residential uses (likely MUB). The current future 
land use designation does not support this type of zone change. A future land use update would 
benefit the community by allowing the expansion of residential uses in the area, thus addressing 
an issue not foreseen in the planning process. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 1. 
 
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, and the 
goals and policies of the Plan.  
 
The subject property is adjacent to several properties with Mixed-use Commercial and Urban 
Residential designations. Furthermore, the proposed amendment to mixed-use commercial 
would support the existing businesses and residences in the area as the designation calls for a 
mix of commercial and residential uses. The proposed amendment is consistent with the goals 
and policies of PlanCheyenne, as discussed in more detail below. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 2. 
   
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate impacts on 
transportation, services, and facilities.  
 
Transportation: 
The transportation network in this area of the City is currently developed. All impacts to the 
transportation network will be addressed through the platting and site plan processes. 
 
Services: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on provision of services to the subject 
property. Service providers, such as the fire department, will be included in platting and site 
planning processes to assess service needs. 
 
Facilities: 
The proposed change will likely not require new facilities outside of the development area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 3. 
 
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on service provision, 
including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, and is compatible with 
existing and planned service provision  

PLN-21-00016 
 
 
January 18, 2022 
 
This land is within City limits and urban facilities and services are available to the subject 
properties. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 4. 
 
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to annex the 
property. 
 
The subject properties are all currently within City limits.   
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 5. 
 .   
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical expansion of 
the Urban Services Area. 
 
This land is currently within the Urban Services Area and does not expand the current area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 6. 
 
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation neither intended 
nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan. 
 
Strict adherence to the Plan may result in unintended consequences. The existing residences are 
not permitted to expand in their current zoning designation. The current future land use 
designation does not support zoning map amendments to permit these residences to rezone to 
a district in which they would be a permitted use. Furthermore, the current lot sizes of the 
residential properties in the area are not conducive to commercial development. The future 
land use amendment to Mixed-use Commercial acknowledges the existing residential 
development in the area without being detrimental to the existing commercial development in 
the area as it permits both. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 7. 
 
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will promote the public 
welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne and the elements 
thereof. 
 
Staff is satisfied that the proposed application will not have any adverse effects on public 
welfare. Furthermore, the application accomplishes the following policies contained within 
PlanCheyenne: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.B. Flexibility to Market Demands: This goal notes that the Future Land Use 
Plan does not predetermine all uses on individual parcels and is meant to provide 
flexibility. The future land use map amendment to MU-C will accommodate existing 
development without being detrimental to existing conditions. 
• 
Policy 1.4.A. Infill Opportunities: The future land use map amendment may facilitate 
infill opportunities for residential expansion. The community is currently facing a 
housing shortage and allowing residential in more places and providing more 
opportunities for residential expansion will aid in addressing the shortage. 

PLN-21-00016 
January 18, 2022 
•
Policy 2.1.A. Enhance and Stabilize Existing Neighborhoods: This amendment
recognizes the existing character of the neighborhood as a mix of residential and
commercial uses.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 8. 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
No other plans are applicable for this area.  
CONCLUSION: 
Staff recommends a MU-C Land Use Category for this property because it acknowledges the 
existing character of the neighborhood. The area is made up of residential and commercial uses 
and the current future land use designation only accommodates the commercial uses. A future 
land use amendment to MU-C will support future rezoning initiatives in the area that permit the 
expansion of residential uses in the area while allowing the continuance of commercial uses. 
ALTERNATIVES: 
1.
Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan as submitted by the applicant and
expanded upon by staff (Staff Recommendation).
2.
Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan subject to modifications.
3.
Deny the Resolution to not amend the Land Use Plan.  The Planning Commission must conclude
that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings for denial
must be stated.
4.
Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific 
reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan for 66 lots generally 
located west of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway  
from CB (Community Business) to MU-C (Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis).

Application

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
February 3, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Investment Policy Statement 
 
 
On January 25, 2022, the City’s Investment Committee, which is comprised of the Mayor, City 
Treasurer, City Council President and Finance Committee Chair, voted to recommend approval of 
revisions made to the City’s Investment Policy Statement.   
The City is fortunate to have Mr. Patrick Fleming, the Chief Investment Officer for the State of 
Wyoming, and Mr. Jeffrey Prince, a local investment advisor, to serve as non-voting  members of 
this Committee.  They provided their investment expertise in making suggested changes to the 
policy statement.  In addition, the City’s new Investment Advisor, Federated Hermes, reviewed 
the City’s policy statement to ensure we comply with Wyoming State Statutes.   
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.

RESOLUTION NO.________ 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE CITY OF CHEYENNE TO 
APPROVE AND FILE THE WYOMING DIVISION OF VICTIM 
SERVICES FUNDING APPLICATION STATE FISCAL YEAR 2023 AND 
2024, JULY 1, 2022 TO JUNE 30, 2024.” 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. Ann. § 1-40-118, all grantees receiving VOCA, 
VAWA and FVSPA funds are required to submit additional subgrantee reports to DVS in 
accordance with deadlines established through the Office on Violence Against Women and the 
Family Violence Prevention and Services Program; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program will 
apply for funds to provide basic, direct services to victims of crime and be in good standing with 
the Wyoming Division of Victim Services Rules. Awarded applications are funded with one, or a 
combination of, State Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault funds, State Victim/Witness funds, 
State Child Advocacy funds, State Salary funds, Federal Violence Against Women Act (VAWA) 
Services, Training, Law Enforcement Officers, and Prosecution (STOP) funds, Sexual Assault 
Services Program (SASP) funds, federal Victim of Crime Act (VOCA) funds and Federal Family 
Violence Prevention Services Act (FVPSA) funds; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance 
Program will submit the application through the WyoGrants System by Tuesday, March 15, 2022 
for State Fiscal Years 2023 and 2024; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance 
Program will be required to submit a DUNS number and register the DUNS number with the 
System for Award Management (SAM) database; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will be required to meet a 20% cash or in-kind match.  
Applicants will not be required to provide match information at the time of application.  Upon 
receipt of the award notice, applicants will be required to submit a final detailed budget, including 
match, based on actual award amounts as part of their award stipulations and submit a match 
certification form; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, the City of Cheyenne, 
Cheyenne Police Department Victims Assistance, understand the long-term obligations of the 
Wyoming Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2023 and 2024, July 
1, 2022 to June 30, 2024. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body approves the funding application and 
application process through the Wyoming Division of Victim Services State Fiscal Year 2023 and 
2024 (July 1, 2022 to June 30, 2022.) 
By Stefanie Boster at 8:27 pm, Feb 08, 2022

 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor and City Clerk are authorized to execute 
all documents relating to the Wyoming Division of Victim Services State Fiscal Year 2023 and 
2024 (July 1, 2022 to June 30, 2024.)

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00454: Sweetgrass, 9th Filing Preliminary Plat (Expedited 
Request) 
REQUEST: 
A preliminary plat proposing to replat Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass 1st Filing. 
LOCATION: 
South of Murray Road and West of Sweetgrass Drive.  
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning 
OWNER: 
Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC 
ZONING: 
Sweetgrass PUD (Ordinances 4232, 4286) 
PURPOSE: 
A preliminary plat proposing replating of All of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st 
Filing to separate proposed multi-family onto fee-simple individual lots. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend approval of Sweetgrass 9th Filing Preliminary Plat, subject to staff’s recommended 
conditions, noting that as conditioned, the project meets the review criteria for approval, advancing the 
Final Plat to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 7.70-acre parcel of land to create 34 lots for 
multi-family development. There will be one large common lot, three open space lots and 30 fee simple lots 
for the multi-family buildings. The land is currently vacant.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is zoned as part of the Sweetgrass PUD.  The Sweetgrass PUD is generally described as “a 
±2,349.5-acre community envisioned as a distinctive mixed-use master planned village, integrating 
retail, entertainment, lodging, employment opportunities, and variety of intimately scaled residential 
neighborhoods.”  The district generally allows a broad range of uses. The HDR (High Density Residential) 
land use category is located in close proximity to Laramie County Community College and planned retail 
/ commercial development along College Drive. These parcels are intended to provide for residential 
development to support the student and employee housing needs associated with the community 
college and surrounding community in the form of apartments, townhomes, and more affordable single 
family detached dwellings. To promote greater diversity, lower density development, such as that 
permitted within MDR and LDR districts, is permitted within HDR districts.

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Expedited Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d and UDC 2.1.3.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with a 
residential use. This plat proposes a replat of an existing lot to allow for a multi-family 
development with each building on its own fee simple lot.  
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 2 of PlanCheyenne encourages new residential development to be compatible and 
connected with existing residential neighborhoods. The proposed plat is a replat of an existing 
lot that is adjacent to other residential types and was planned to be a multi-family development. 
 
Foundation 6 of PlanCheyenne encourages the creation of open space and trails and the 
preservation of significant natural resources. The plat proposes dedicated open space lots that 
will be a part of an integrated trail system throughout Sweetgrass along with internal open 
space areas for the residents of the development. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This is a replat of an existing lot with in Sweetgrass, 1st Filing. It does not propose any additional 
public streets and is already adjacent to an existing street network. Utilities and facilities are 
already within the area due to the existing developments in the area. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes replating all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing. The plat proposes private 
drives and will not be dedicating any additional roads or right-of-way. There will be an access 
into the development along Murray Road and an access along Sweetgrass Drive. This lot is 
already within an established street network.  
 
 

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
Fees were paid with the original plat for Sweetgrass, 1st Filing. The required fees for the 7.70 
acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$13,825 ($350 per acre) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
$13,825 Credit 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$13,825 ($350 per acre) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
$13,825 Credit 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$0.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Open Space: 
Open Space parcels were provided with Sweetgrass, 1st Filing. This replat does propose several 
additional open space lots to provide additional green space for the residents.   
  
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. The Sweetgrass PUD calls this portion of the 
2,000 plus acre Sweetgrass site, part of phase 1 through 3 of 10.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A preliminary Drainage Report was submitted and reviewed. It was found that the plan meets 
the intent of the preliminary drainage report requirements contained in Article 3 of the 
Cheyenne UDC. A final drainage report specific to this property will be submitted with the EPR.   
 
Traffic: 
A Traffic Impact Study Letter update was submitted. The proposed platting action was reviewed 
and found to be within the scope of the original Traffic Report based on the updated letter.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is one of the HDR zones of the PUD. The HDR land use category is located in 
close proximity to Laramie County Community College and planned retail / commercial 
development along College Drive. These parcels are intended to provide for residential 
development to support the student and employee housing needs associated with the 
community college and surrounding community in the form of apartments, townhomes, and 
more affordable single family detached dwellings. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within MDR and LDR districts, is permitted within HDR 
districts. All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The PUD requires common lots to be owned, developed, improved, managed, and/or 
maintained by homeowners’ associations, architectural review board(s), community 
development districts, and/or similar entities (PUD Section 1, Subsection G). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is in conformance with the design and layout anticipated for this parcel by the 
Sweetgrass PUD. Adjacent Sweetgrass developments were designed and laid out with this 
development as an integral part of the broader development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or 
general welfare.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff has recommended 
conditions to the plat. These conditions are summarized in the staff recommendation. While this 
condition does not pose a significant engineering or planning issue, it should be addressed prior 
to the Governing Body’s consideration of the Final Plat.  
 
The application complies with conditions for this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.    
  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Technical corrections (some resolved by map revisions) 
2. City Parcel Tract H was included in the plat without City Approval (removed and 
resolved by map revisions) 
3. This is parcel BB on the PUD map - therefore the provisions of the HDR zone apply. 
HDR zoning requires 0.025 acres of PARK per unit for the first 100 units; PUD 4.A.8.d.i 
(Parks are more than just open areas - PUD 3.A.10.b). Please demonstrate where and 
how parcel BB is providing 2.5 acres of PARK area. If not already provided by other 
areas within parcel BB, please demonstrate how it will be provided with this plat. 
(After discussions with the applicant, some space will be provided on the plat and the 

UDC-21-00454  
 
 
February 7, 2022 
remainder will be paid as a fee-in-lieu per PUD allowance to be put toward the 
community park that will be built in Sweetgrass) 
4. Black Hills Energy requested an easement for utilities (resolved by map revisions) 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets all review criteria as filed with conditions. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, or 
decide contrary to staff’s recommendation.    In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall relate 
to applicable review criteria.  In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat subject to the following conditions: 
1. The interior lots have no measurement to the exterior boundary. A tie of some sort will be 
required from the exterior boundary to the interior lots. 
2. The dimension on the east side of Lot 27 will be provided. 
3. The open space lots will be renamed to tracts per PUD 3.A.10.a.iii.

1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_10763861.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: A resolution adopting a fee schedule for planning and development projects 
Date: 
February 14, 2022  
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a fee schedule for development-related applications in the Planning and 
Development Department.   Pursuant to section 1.3.7 of the Unified Development Code, fees are 
adopted by resolution.      
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 
 
February 14, 2022 
• 
Finance Committee 
 
 
February 23, 2022 
• 
Final Reading consideration 
 
February 28, 2022 
 
Summary: 
 
Fees related to applications within the Planning and Development Department were last updated over 
23 years ago on October 25, 1993. The current fees do not adequately cover costs associated with 
development actions and the proposed fees are designed to recover most costs associated with 
development actions.  
 
Staff began working on the revised fee schedule in early 2021 and in developing the fee schedule, the 
following factors were considered: 
• 
Fees of other communities in the region 
• 
Staff management and review time  
• 
Publication costs 
• 
Legal advertisement costs 
• 
Office overhead (10% of staff management and review)  
• 
Data storage and access (Innoprise / Citizen Access) 
• 
Number and complexity of item 
• 
Compliance with requirement to make specific applications 
• 
Degree in which the specific application is a public service 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

Fees of other communities in the region 
Staff’s initial task was to review peer nearby jurisdictions to determine if our fees needed adjustment. 
Communities reviewed included Casper, Fort Collins, Gillette, Kimball, Laramie, Loveland, Timnath, and 
Wellington.  Staff also looked at the nearby counties of Albany, Campbell, Laramie, Larimer, Natrona, 
and Weld. It was noted that most communities had different types of fees, methodologies, and 
application classifications that prevented a simple and straightforward comparison.  One thing was for 
certain though, Cheyenne’s development-related fees were typically the lowest in the region.  For 
example, a preliminary plat in Greeley had an application fee of $3,000 + $10 per lot and Casper’s 
preliminary plat fee was a flat $1,000 compared to our $175 fee.  Site Plans in Casper ranged between 
$600-$2,000 and Loveland were between $800-$2,400 compared to our $100 fee.  This triggered a need 
to investigate the costs associated with development review.        
Staff Management and Review Time 
This factor included surveying all employees included in the development review process. Utilized were 
the average of reported hours per project multiplied by position’s average hourly salary plus taxes, 
worker’s comp, benefits, etc. Review time was averaged for all project types.  For example, if three 
items of one type were reviewed over the period of a year by three planners, we would utilize the 
average time all planners took to review that project and account for their average hourly costs to 
determine a typical rate.     
Publication Costs 
The department has worked to reduce publication costs over the course of the year in working with 
other departments to limit the publication of approved ordinances to the minimum necessary under 
state statute.  Still, several applications (Annexation and Street Vacation) do require publication of the 
full ordinance which generally ranges between $300 and $600 each. Ordinances that may be published 
by title and summary only have been reduced to a cost of approximately $50 each. This fee schedule 
includes publication costs associated with approved ordinances.         
Legal Advertisement Costs 
The department has reduced costs associated with legal advertisements over the course of the year by 
consolidating advertisements for projects and removing irrelevant information. Annexations require 
multiple legal advertisements and specific information average approximately $180 while regular 
Planning Commission items average $30 each.  This fee schedule includes publication costs associated 
with the specific development applications.       
Office Overhead (10% of Staff Management and Review) 
Included as a cost to process an application, was a 10% fee for office overhead ranging between $40 and 
$150, and averaging $66.73 per project.  In 2021, 273 applications would have been subject to this fee, 
and it is estimated that at the current rate it would produce approximately $18,217.29 in annual 
revenue to capture overhead costs. This was not applied to building permit, sign permits, and several 
other application types.  This element of the fee will offset office furniture, supplies, equipment, 
printers, etc. 

Data storage and Access (Innoprise / Citizen Access) 
Presently the City utilizes Harris’s ERP Community Development module (Innoprise/CommDev).   The 
Department represents 1/11th of the Help Desk service totaling a proportionate cost of $529.45 
annually. The Planning and Zoning Module has an annual cost of $11,649.72 which has been equally 
divided into all project types utilizing this system for planning and zoning purposes.  This software is 
used for project tracking, project review, citizen access, and record retention.  The total cost for 
development-related use of this software and access to the help desk is $12,179.17 annually. If a new 
system is implemented, staff will request to modify the fee schedule once again to reflect any cost 
changes.   
Number and complexity of item 
Staff evaluated all types of applications that were received in the department and their level of 
complexity and knowledge necessary to review/complete.  Frequent applications included Site Plans 
(74), Zoning Letter requests (57), Administrative Adjustments (42), as well as action required on items 
forwarded for review by the Building Division including specific building permits (537) and sign permits 
(104). Several repetitive projects were given a flat fee, like Building Permits, and others were given a 
quantitative based fee, like sign permits which are proposed to be calculated on a $10 per sign basis. 
More complex projects oftentimes warranted slight bumps in a fee due to the expertise needed while 
other ones warranted a bump downward and could be considered a public service.      
Compliance with Requirement to Make Specific Applications 
Several City processes have been problematic to adequately capture.  For example, Home Occupations 
exist throughout the City, however we only have record 229 home occupations having been approved 
since 2012.  We are certain that more exist, but do not want to create a fee that would discourage 
voluntary compliance and present itself as a barrier.  Likewise, temporary signage runs rampant 
throughout the City. The Development Division manages all temporary signs, however the only ones 
that are typically reviewed are for temporary placement of signs by nonprofits and community 
organizations at designated locations within City parks. This fee was set at zero to not penalize the 
people lawfully erecting temporary signage which is very few.  Additionally, temporary signs are not 
tracked in our data base and implementation of a nominal fee would likely create more steps, 
complicate a process, and add more work associated with that process resulting in a loss of productivity 
and a negative monetary effect on the department.       
Degree in which the specific application is a public service 
A large portion of the City Planning and Development Department is to provide effective, and efficient 
professional planning services to the community.  In 2021 the department received 229 inquiries and 
hosted 129 preapplication meetings.   These services represent 24.8% of the development-related tasks 
managed annually by a planner. Inquiries typically are informational requests from developers or 
residents inquiring about allowances on their property or a prospective property.  Pre-application 
meetings are intended to identify any major red flags with a proposed development and most all 
involved in the development review process attend those meetings on a weekly basis. Pre-application 
meetings have no fee and represent an approximate $1,200 value to the developer. Staff believes that 
these meetings clarify expectations for future development and ultimately reduce potential increased 
review time, development delays, and staff time spent on applications once formally submitted.  

Current Fee Schedule: 
Below is the current fee schedule that was derived from Resolution No. 3451 which was adopted on 
October 25, 1993. Several additional fees have been added over time including Telecommunication 
Towers and Special Use Permits.   Those remain unchanged in the new fee schedule.   
Proposed Fee Schedule: 
The proposed fee schedule is attached to the Resolution as Attachment A.   Only two fees are being 
carried over from the previous fee schedule - Telecommunication Towers and Special Use Permits. As 
previously indicated in this memorandum the fees were derived by considering a variety of factors.  
Attached to this memorandum is an attachment labeled “Application Cost Summary” summarizing the 
costs associated with specific development review.  Staff notes that the initial subdivision calculations 
were based on lot quantity ranges, not a base fee plus fee per lot basis.  Upon evaluation of lots platted 
with subdivision types, staff determined that a base plus per lot fee resulted in essentially the same 
cost-recovery but more equitably distributed the financial burden of a subdivision review to developers 
based on their plat size.  Below is a summary of the lots associated with subdivision types.   
Subdivision Type Summary

For example, under this scenario, the typical Final Plat application would be $1,915 dollars [$700 base 
fee + $1,215 (81 lots x $15)]. The attached sheet showed a cost of $3,000 for any subdivision over 35 lots 
which has since been revised to a per lot basis. 

Telecommunication Safe Harbor Amounts: 
Telecommunications are regulated by the Federal Communications Commission (FCC), and they have 
established specific rates by order at which telecommunication providers can be charged for 
development applications and annual leases.  The safe harbor amounts are: 
•
$500 for a single up-front application that includes up to five small wireless facilities, with an
additional $100 for each small wireless facility beyond five,
•
$270 per small wireless facility per year for all recurring fees, including any possible ROW access
fee or fee for attachment to municipally owned structures in the ROW, and
•
$1,000 for nonrecurring fees for a new pole.
The FCC views these amounts as safe harbors because it believes they are low enough that no carrier 
would challenge them if they were imposed unilaterally in a local government’s regulations. Nothing in 
the FCC’s overarching order prevents a local government from charging higher fees. However, under the 
FCC's framework, if a carrier files a lawsuit challenging the fees imposed by a local government, the 
burden would be on the local government to demonstrate that the amount is a reasonable 
approximation of its costs and that its costs are reasonable. The FCC did not specify a methodology for 
calculating cost, or what expenses could be included. To this date, we have not had specific applications 
to analyze exact costs and have opted to keep our existing fees for large macro-cell towers and adopt 
the safe harbor amounts for small cell and distributed antenna systems (DAS).  
Projected Revenue Comparison to Expenses: 
The Planning and Development divisions are comprised of seven employees with three employees 
mostly responsible for processing development-related requests.  The Development Division does have 
overlap into the Planning Division, Parks and Greenway Division, and Office Manager and Planning and 
Development Director positions. Total salaries plus all taxes and benefits for the three primary planners 
are projected to total $232,037.64 in FY2023 based on current funding.  Projected revenue based on this 
fee schedule is $255,955.00 which should account for the planners responsible for current development 
review and a portion of the overflow to the others within the department, including the Planner II Long 
Range, Parks and Greenway Manager, Office Manager, and the Director.  Revenue in 2021 was 
approximately 
$43,025.    The Department is seeking the Senior Planner Position be reinstated which will cost 
approximately $114, 900 for salary plus benefits.   Addition of the new staff member will likely result in 
additional hours to review individual applications and give existing staff more time to focus on 
individual projects and eliminate potential for error or oversites.   
The entire department’s anticipated FY 2023 payroll budget is $895,917.80 (with Senior Planner added) 
($780,994.00 with no new positions) compared to FY 2022’s $741,573.16 payroll budget. Staff will 
continue to monitor costs associated with development review and may  
Staff Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the Governing Body adopt the resolution to establish a new Planning and 
Development Fee Schedule.

RESOLUTION NO. 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION ADOPTING A FEE SCHEDULE FOR PLANNING AND 
DEVELOPMENT PROJECTS." 
WHEREAS, on January 23, 2012, the Governing Body approved Ordinance 3943 creating 
the Unified Development Code (UDC); and   
WHEREAS, on October 25, 1993, the Governing Body, jointly with the County 
Commissioners, adopted Resolution 3451 creating a schedule of fees related to development and 
subdivision applications; and  
WHEREAS, the costs of conducting business has increased and the established fees do not 
reflect costs associated with development actions or the services offered by the City; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to UDC Section 1.3.7 the City may charge fees to defray some or all 
of the cost of processing land development applications; and   
WHEREAS, adoption of a fee schedule is necessary to defray the costs associated with 
processing land development applications; and   
WHEREAS, staff has conducted internal research and surveys to measure costs associated 
with development, including but not limited to, costs associated with staff review time, legal notice, 
ordinance publication, data storage, development related software, and data storage; and 
WHEREAS, staff recognizes that some services of the department are a public service in the 
interest of the public safety, health and welfare of the Cheyenne community and fees to defray total 
costs may not be necessary and appropriate and may be nominal to encourage compliance; and   
WHEREAS, a fee schedule has been created and in attached to this resolution as Attachment 
A consisting of one (1) page which is attached hereto and incorporated herein.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the fee schedule shown in Attachment A of this resolution 
is hereby adopted and all previous development fees are hereby replaced.    
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective March 31, 
2022, and shall apply to all applicable applications filed on or after the date. 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS 28TH DAY OF FEBRUARY 2022. 
Patrick J. Collins, Mayor 
(Seal) 
ATTEST: 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
By Stefanie Boster at 9:57 am, Feb 09, 2022

RESOLUTION NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION FOR GOVERNING BODY APPROVAL OF A ONE-
YEAR 
BRONZE 
BOOT 
SPONSOR 
RELATIONSHIP 
BETWEEN 
CHEYENNE FRONTIER DAYS AND MILLER/COORS FOR 2022.     
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne Frontier Days relies heavily on sponsors/partners to help produce 
their event; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming State Statute § 12-5-402 allows a licensing authority to approve 
malt beverage industry representatives to furnish, give or lend money or other things of value to 
malt beverage license to support annual community events that are open to the public if certain 
requirements are met; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, those statutory requirements are met in this instance as Cheyenne Frontier 
Days is a nonprofit corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, is qualified as 
a tax-exempt organization under the Internal Revenue Code and has been in continuous operation 
for a period of not less than two years. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body approves a one-year Bronze 
Boot Sponsor relationship between Cheyenne Frontier Days and Miller/Coors for 2022.       
 
  NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this approval is specific 
only to the event known as Cheyenne Frontier Days and would apply to the following time frame:  
July 22-31, 2022.   
 
  NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this Resolution shall be 
effective upon passage and execution by the Mayor and City Clerk.     
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS ______ DAY OF ________________, 
2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
_________________________________________  
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: A Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between the City of Cheyenne and Doran 
Lummis to authorize filing of platting application(s) for City-owned lands for the purpose of 
access and future development. 
Date: 
January 24, 2022   
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a memorandum of understanding (MOU) between the City and Doran 
Lummis to allow an application to replat City-owned land within the Sweetgrass 2nd Filing Plat in 
association with a replat of Sweetgrass 6th Filing.  Pursuant to chapter 2.1 of the Unified Development 
Code, applications for plats may only be initiated by a landowner or authorized agent. This MOU 
authorizes application submittal and review. This MOU shall not be considered as the City granting 
approval of any subsequent development application(s) involving this land. Development of any right-of-
way platted will be the responsibility of the applicant or their assignee, not the City.   
 
Under the MOU, a portion of the City property may be converted to right-of-way for access purposes. 
The right-of-way location is anticipated to replace the utility, access, and construction easement across 
Tract U that the Governing Body approved on July 14, 2021. That easement has not been recorded with 
the County Clerk at this time. Final alignments will be subject to review and approval through the 
platting process. 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

Applicant’s Land, 
Lot 100, 6th Filing 
Applicant’s Land, Lot 
200, 6th Filing

Approximate ROW Location

Page 1 of  7 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (City), located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Doran Lummis, an individual, of 1825 
Campstool Road, Cheyenne, WY 82007 (Applicant).  The City and Applicant are collectively 
referred to as “Party” or “Parties.”  This MOU shall become effective upon the date of the last 
signature affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant intends to replat Lots 100 and 200, Block 15 of Sweetgrass 6th 
Filing and Public Greenway Tract U of Sweetgrass 2nd Filing; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City is owner of Public Greenway Tract U of Sweetgrass 2nd Filing; and  
 
WHEREAS, the intention of City involvement in the plat is to plat a right-of-way for 
access to replatted Lots 100 and 200, Block 15 of Sweetgrass 6th Filing; and   
WHEREAS, pursuant to chapter 2.1 of the Unified Development Code, applications for a 
division of land may be initiated by the property owner(s) of all property subject to the 
application, or the property owner(s)’s authorized agent; and 
WHEREAS, City authorization to allow the Applicant to file an application for a division 
of land involving City-owned lands is required pursuant to chapter 2.1 of the Unified 
Development Code; and 
WHEREAS, City authorization allows the Applicant to plat City-owned lands for the 
purpose of access and future development; and 
WHEREAS, this agreement shall not be construed as the City granting approval of the 
development application(s) authorized herein. 
By Stefanie Boster at 12:08 pm, Jan 19, 2022

 
Page 2 of  7 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Recitals Incorporated.  The foregoing recitals are incorporated herein by reference 
into this Agreement. 
2. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to allow replatting of All of Lot 1 & 2, 
Block 1, inclusive, P.A.S. Subdivision, First Filing and platting of currently unplatted City land 
immediately east of Cheyenne Ice & Events Center Second Filing plat;  
 
3. 
Term. This MOU shall commence on the last date executed by the duly 
authorized representatives of the Parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect 
until February 14, 2024, for a period of two (2) years. 
 
4. 
Payments.  Neither Party shall have any obligation to make any payment of any 
kind to the other Party under this MOU. 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of Applicant. Applicant shall: 
a. 
File necessary division of land applications and pay required fees in 
accordance with City standards. 
 
 
b. 
May plat one street right-of-way through City-owned lands for the 
purposes of establishment of right-of-way. Right-of-way widths and locations shall be at 
the discretion of the City.  
d. 
Be responsible for all public improvements and surveying related to 
division of land applications. 
 
6. 
Responsibilities of the City. The City shall: 
a. (Mayor) Sign necessary authorizations for the Applicant to proceed with 
platting applications related to the lands mentioned herein without delay.   

 
Page 3 of  7 
b.  Assist the Applicant with provision of necessary information regarding the 
platting of City-owned land.  
c.  Allow the Applicant and his/her agents access to City owned lands for carrying 
out necessary actions relating to applications authorized per this agreement. 
 
7. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all Parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No Party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning Party, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the Parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
 e. 
Indemnification.  Applicant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and 
defend the City from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and 
suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, 
which may hereafter arise as a result the performance of the Licensee’s duties, including 

 
Page 4 of  7 
death or bodily injury to any person, destruction or damage to any property, 
contamination of or adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental 
laws, regulations, or orders to the extent caused by (1) Applicant’s breach of any term or 
provision of this Agreement; or (2) any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by 
Applicant, or its employees, in the performance of this Agreement.  Applicant 
acknowledges that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of 
this paragraph. 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 

 
Page 5 of  7 
MOU shall operate only between the Parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the Parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the Parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 
Parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only parties signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a Party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the Parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the Parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any Party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other Party. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 

 
Page 6 of  7 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the Party either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any Party may terminate its participation in this MOU, with 
or without cause, by providing thirty (30) days written notice to the other Party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the Parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
8. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the Parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU. 

 
Page 7 of  7

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 9, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Veolia ES 
Technical Solutions, LLC, for the Hazardous Waste Disposal Services project 
______________________________________________ 
Two (2) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #4-22, for the 
Hazardous Waste Disposal Services Project. 
 
The responsive proposals were received from:  
1. 
AET Environmental.  Denver, CO. 
2. 
Veolia ES Technical Solutions, LLC.  Henderson, CO 
 
One vendor listed below, failed to Acknowledge receipt of the One (1) issued Addendum, therefore 
their proposal was not scored. 
1. Clean Harbors Environmental Services, Inc.  Norwell, MA.  (NOT EVALUATED) 
 
Upon review of the two (2) proposals by the evaluation panel (consisting of 3 members), the 
evaluation panel has recommended acceptance of the proposal submitted by Veolia ES Technical 
Solutions, LLC, for a project award amount not-to-exceed $200,000.00.  This Agreement is based 
on actual quantities disposed, and is billed in accordance with the Fee Schedule attached as Exhibit 
A, to the Professional Services Agreement.  If future Contract renewals are executed after the 
original term of the Agreement ends, additional funds may need to be added as well. 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications based review and scoring of proposals.  Veolia ES 
Technical Solutions, LLC, submitted the highest scoring proposal, therefore their sealed pricing 
envelope was opened & negotiated. 
 
Funding:   
023-14-1423-43-21301 (Solid Waste Fund / Professional Services) 
 
$200,000.00

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., Deputy City Engineer 
 
DATE: 
February 14, 2022 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement for Professional Land Surveying Services. 
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City and Jones Land 
Surveying, Inc. for surveying services for the City of Cheyenne. 
  
Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during 
design/review operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this 
agreement will provide the required services to complete the surveying related tasks necessary for 
the review of City and developer projects. 
 
City funding for these services will come from 2019 – 2022 1% sales tax. The total professional 
services will be a cost not-to-exceed $25,000.00. We respectfully request your approval of this 
professional services agreement. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

1 
 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Professional Land Surveying Services 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2022, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Jones Land Surveying, Inc., 6750 Say Kally Rd, Cheyenne, WY 82009 
(“Consultant”). 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by January 30, 2023, as stated 
in Exhibit A. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Twenty-
Five Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00). 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant, 
the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting 
such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.
